# Paperboat launch checklist

The **only** progress tracker. PLAN.md explains each item (decisions, scope, where to start);
this file records whether it works. Work top to bottom, one section at a time: a feature is
done when **all** its scenario rows and command rows are `[x]` on L and M (Windows after launch, PLAN 1.1; W cells are not in the launch gate). Then the next.

## How to mark

- Section **X. Cross-platform** is different: its three cells are platform pairs (L↔M, L↔W, M↔W), each `[x]` only when verified in both directions between two real devices.

- Cells: **L** = Linux (hp), **M** = macOS (apple), **W** = Windows (victus). For S-sections
  (groundwork, infrastructure, release) only the first cell is used.
- `[ ]` not verified · `[~]` in progress · `[x]` verified on the real device · `[!]` fails
  (write the failure in Evidence) · `n/a` impossible on that platform (say why in Evidence).
- Mark `[x]` **only** with evidence: a smoke-suite test id with its results file
  (`smoke:<id> results/<file>.json`), or the commit plus exact command and result summary
  for a manual check (`manual: <commit> <what was run> → <result>`).
- When a fix changes code, re-verify every `[x]` row of the affected features on all
  platforms; any row that breaks becomes `[!]` and is fixed first (regressions first).
- Commit and push this file together with the work it records.

## Command rows: what `[x]` means

A command row is `[x]` on a platform only when, on that platform: (1) its main use works
against real machines and server with a correct result; (2) every flag in
`cli/docs/cli/<command>.md` behaves as documented (or the doc is fixed); (3) `--json` output,
where supported, is valid; (4) errors are clear for invalid input, missing permission, target
offline and not signed in; (5) `--help` is accurate; (6) it is in the smoke suite when it can
be automated. Hidden/internal commands (e.g. `pb daemon …`) are verified through the flows
that use them; say which in Evidence.

| F9-01 | `https://<port>.<m>.<local domain>` and `https://<m>.<local domain>:<port>` open with trusted HTTPS from every client platform — all pairs; and when this needs an interactive operating-system trust approval, the install still completes, says trust is pending and gives the exact command to finish it. | [x] | [ ] | [ ] | 1.4c edge browser/private/public TCP use shared admission lifetime and revocation owner; short-TTL duplex tests preserve explicit cuts and strict new admission. BrowserAccessPostgres PASS0.46s; private ingress signer tests PASS. Device qualification pending.  shared/machineservices producer moved; real machine authority PostgreSQL PASS0.46s and owner race PASS; Lane A shared consumers/device verification pending.  Runtime carrier/edge placement wire types consolidated; real attachment PG PASS0.89s and affected race PASS; A shared consumer and batch smoke pending.  Final shared carrier PostgreSQL owner PASS1.01s and deployed edge15.5/server15.13; smoke pending. Raw TCP stays on the machine address, documented in cli.md. Per-platform trust mechanism (was F1-06): macOS shows a GUI certificate-trust prompt; Linux needs none (Lane C audit 2026-10-21); Windows uses its certificate store, checked after launch (PLAN 1.1). [source row F1-06: L n/a, M [x], W [ ]] Actual Apple dev-2b23fd2 own pb install --json succeeds with browser_https:trust_pending and warning: Recovery: retry `pb install` and approve the operating system certificate trust request. Final service ready, version/doctor/native exec/terminal/SSH/transfer/preview pass. Automated TestMachineInstallSucceedsWhenGuardTrustIsPending plus existing guard-reuse trust check; private receipt /tmp/paperboat-lane-a-apple-install.json. No certificate approval bypass. Linux not applicable; W deferred PLAN1.1. Linux n/a: the browser HTTPS trust prompt that needs GUI approval is macOS-only; Linux has no such step (Lane C 2026-10-21, per coordinator). Windows: its certificate-store approval, if it needs one, is checked after launch (PLAN 1.1); the row counts for W. |
| F9-02 | Raw TCP via `127.100.x.x` and `pb access machine <m> --port <p> --listen 127.0.0.1:<q>`. | [x] | [x] | [ ] | M: raw assigned-address Apple→HP/Apple PASS141445/141903; explicit Apple→HP15s/Apple→Apple19s PASS on verified f874b97, receipt20261016T155257Z; exact binary bytes and TCP half-close. f874b97 waits for exact port publication before granting (controlled403 before publication/authorized afterward; production constructor RED0.105s/GREEN race4.338s, vet PASS). HP→Apple PASS26s; HP-self listener deadline repeats32/33s (155257/155731), L remains[!] for native self-connect setup after publication; no check weakened.  Qualified Ubuntu24 dev46c/server18.14 existing private_access origin/probe: assigned address exact bytes/half-close PASS, explicit access-machine probe exit1; origin cleaned. Known Linux explicit-access blocker therefore remains. L: local_access.py pbvm2->hp and pbvm2->self (dev-3a3c7de): private-address PASS 10.9s/15.0s, explicit-listen PASS 4.3s/5.9s (exact bytes, half-close, foreground owner). Root cause of the old Linux failure: pb access machine opened a second peer session on the daemon's identity and both relays rejected it; fixed in 3a3c7de (pb access machine now dials through the daemon), tests TestMachineServiceWait*, TestDaemonAccessRuntimeDials*. |

Waits: the device proof needs a second signed-in account with its own daemon (CLI requests are now bound to the daemon account, db9cb466); team share/grant/revoke harness (team_machines.py) has no teammate daemon. Server-side authorization and revocation proofs above PASS. L live 2026-10-21 (server 2026.10.20.7, pbvm2-4 owner on pprbtdev, ubuntu24 dev-141051aa member on a second account, team paperboat-smoke-paid): tools/device-smoke/private_access_permission.py PASS. Setting off with the grant: refused with the team-setting message; setting on with a grant lacking private_access: refused with the permission message; setting on with the grant: bytes echo across accounts; turning the setting off closes the held flow in 4.0 s and a new attempt is refused (setting message); revoking the grant closes it in 4.2 s and a new attempt is refused (permission message). Setting and sharing restored after the run. Fixes needed on the way: the daemon topology lists machines a team shared with private_access (shared_machines.go), the accessor no longer reads the owner-private endpoint certificate (ResolveLocal, also unblocks the shared-terminal join), and pb access machine names the denial reason. |

| F13-07 | A plus-address sign-up is refused when the canonical address has an account: `you+x@any.domain` gets "An account for you@any.domain already exists. Sign in with that address." (lowercased, first `+tag` stripped). No silent merge: on non-Gmail domains a +tag can be a different mailbox, so merging would be an account-takeover risk (user decision 2026-10-23, option a). A plus address with no canonical account signs up normally. Team invitations match the same canonical way. Enforced server-side (WorkOS registration Action, signed). | [x] | [ ] | [ ] | Live 2026-10-23 (Lane O, server 2026.10.23.44+, console, incognito): sign up with pprbtdev+f13@gmail.com → refused at the WorkOS registration Action with "An account for pprbtdev@gmail.com already exists. Sign in with that address."; no WorkOS user created. Automated checks: TestWorkOSRegistrationActionDeniesAnAliasOfAnExistingAccount, TestWorkOSRegistrationActionAllowsAnythingElse, TestWorkOSRegistrationActionRefusesUnsignedStaleAndMalformedRequests, TestWorkOSRegistrationActionNeverDeniesOnAnInternalError (httpapi), TestExistingAccountForAliasPostgres, TestWorkOSSignupEmailPolicyPostgres (auth PG). M/W: the flow is the same on every platform (user rule 2026-10-26), so the row counts for M and W; verified on L first. |

## X. Cross-platform

Behaviour that only matters when platforms mix. The three cells are platform **pairs**, not platforms: **L↔M** (Linux and macOS), **L↔W** (Linux and Windows), **M↔W** (macOS and Windows). A pair is `[x]` only when the check passed in **both directions** between two real devices of those platforms (A→B and B→A), with the exact evidence named in the Evidence column; single-platform behaviour stays in F1–F15. Pair devices: L = hp / pbvm* / ubuntu24, M = apple, W = victus. Rows are written from how the code actually works (config sync: `cli/internal/hostruntime/configsync`; layers, path rules and manifest limits are quoted where they decide the check). Windows pairs follow the launch gate rules (PLAN 1.1): they are checked after launch.

**How config sync layers work today (so the rows below match it).** Four sources are merged in this order, later wins per field: the shared file `.paperboat/config-sync.toml` in the repository, its `[os.linux]`, `[os.darwin]` or `[os.windows]` section for the machine's OS, its `[machine.<name>]` section (`<name>` is the machine's Paperboat name from `pb machine list`), then the machine's own file (`pb config sync path`; it may not contain `[os.*]` or `[machine.*]`). Fields: `enabled`, `mode`, `automatic_updates`, `[pull]`/`[push]` (`repository_id`, `url`, `branch`), `[paths."<repository path>"]` (`local_path`, `kind = file|directory`, `include`, `exclude`). `~`, `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `%APPDATA%`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%` and `%USERPROFILE%` expand only when written and available; a missing variable fails. Content is never converted (no line-ending, charset or template step). Only regular files sync (`unsupported_file_type` otherwise); the only mode kept is "executable or not". A `[machine.<name>]` that matches none of the account's machines is a warning in `validate`/`apply` (text and `--json` `warnings`); a machine whose name the daemon does not know yet keeps sync paused instead of guessing (the server sends the name as `machine_alias`, 952fe7b3). There is no per-architecture or per-distribution OS key. Rows X-02 and X-12 record what exists.

| ID | Scenario (what is checked, and how) | L↔M | L↔W | M↔W | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| X-01 | Config sync, same repository file, different destinations per OS: a shared `.paperboat/config-sync.toml` maps `settings.json` with `[os.linux.paths]`, `[os.darwin.paths]` and `[os.windows.paths]` (`~/.config/…`, `~/Library/Application Support/…`, `%APPDATA%/…`) for both `kind = "file"` and `kind = "directory"` (with `include`/`exclude`). On each side `pb config sync validate --json` must show its own resolved `local_path`; an edit on one device arrives byte-identical (sha256) at the other in both directions; a variable that does not exist on that OS fails with the documented error, not a substitute path. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-02 | Config sync precedence and per-device overrides: machine file over the OS section over shared defaults. On each pair, one machine overrides a single field only (its own `local_path`, or `mode = "push_only"`, or `automatic_updates`) and `validate --json` on both shows the override only on that device and inherited values elsewhere; an `[os.*]` or `[machine.*]` section in a machine file is rejected; an empty machine file inherits everything. Per-device section (built; device check pending): put `[machine.<name>]` in the shared file for one pair member and check `validate --json` shows its values only on that machine, ranked between the OS section and the machine file (path rule `source` is `machine_section`); a misspelled name shows a warning; renaming the machine (`pb machine rename`) falls back to the OS/shared values and pauses sync until `pb config sync apply`. Tests: `TestMachineSectionPrecedenceBetweenOSAndMachineFile`, `TestMachineSectionNeedsMachineNameAndKnownSyntax`, `TestConfigMachineRuleSources`. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-03 | Line endings are carried byte for byte: a CRLF file written on W arrives on L and M unchanged (sha256 equal), an LF file on W stays LF, a mixed file keeps its mix, and a final line without newline stays without; Git autocrlf is not involved (embedded Git). An independent edit on each side of a CRLF file merges by the three-way text merge without turning the file into mixed endings; an edit that only changes line endings counts as a change (conflict if the other side also changed). | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-04 | Config sync permissions: a file that is executable on L or M arrives executable on the other Unix side and keeps the executable intent through a Windows machine (which has no POSIX bits) and back; a file authored on W arrives non-executable on L and M; a mapped file that is group or world writable on Unix is refused with `unsafe_permissions` (shown in `pb config status` Skipped) and the Windows equivalent is checked (inherited ACL write access for other principals); local permission bits of an existing destination are kept on replacement. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-05 | Symlinks, junctions and reparse points are never followed or synced: a mapped path that is a symlink (L, M) or a junction/symlink/reparse point (W) is reported `unsupported_file_type`; a link in any ancestor of a destination is refused; a repository file that is a symlink is not materialised as one on the other OS; none of this deletes or changes the link target. Same result on every OS of the pair. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-06 | Case-insensitive and reserved names: two rules whose repository paths differ only by case are rejected as overlapping when the config is validated (same on all OSes, so a repository that works on L cannot silently break on M or W); inside a directory rule, files `A.txt` and `a.txt` are reported as a case collision and not synced to either side; names with `:`, a trailing dot or space, or reserved Windows names (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`–`COM9`, `LPT1`–`LPT9`) are refused in rules on every OS. On W, destination comparison is case-insensitive (a rule pointing at `C:\Users\X\app.json` and `c:\users\x\APP.json` is one path). | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-07 | Names and paths: Unicode file names round-trip on all pairs including macOS NFC/NFD normalisation (a name created decomposed on M must not become a second file or a conflict on L or W); long destination paths on W (over 260 characters) work or fail with a clear message; Windows backslash and drive-letter `local_path` forms are accepted on W only, and the same TOML with a Windows path on a Unix machine fails validation with a clear message instead of creating a literal path. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-08 | Conflicts across OSes: the same line edited on both devices of a pair gives one conflict listed once (not twice) with the same revision on both; `pb config conflict show` and `compare` return both versions byte-exact from the other OS (compare runs over the encrypted peer path); `resolve --keep machine` and `--keep repository` each leave the same bytes on both devices and clear the conflict on both; `force pull` and `force push` do the same across OS; consent and `pb config approve` apply an exact head regardless of OS. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-09 | The config worker runs and is managed on each OS: `pb config sync apply` installs and starts it (systemd user unit on L, launchd agent on M, Windows service on W), status reaches `healthy` after install, reboot or logout, an upgrade keeps it running, and `pb config unassign` removes it. On Ubuntu 24.04 (AppArmor) the unit must not need a user namespace (PrivateTmp off). Checked with a second device of another OS pulling the first device's edits during and after each of these events. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-10 | Repositories across OSes: the same SSH and HTTPS repository connects and validates from each OS with machine-specific credential profiles (native absolute paths for `ssh_key_file` and `known_hosts_file`; `ca_file` for a private CA); local bare-repository paths are per machine (an absolute path from another OS fails with the "this machine cannot reach the config repository" message, not a generic error); `[pull] url` aliases let each OS use its own endpoint for the same registered repository; credential profiles are owner-only (0600 on L and M, owner-only ACL on W) and never printed. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-11 | ENV is delivered and used identically: one set of variables (names with digits and underscores, values with spaces, quotes, `$`, `%`, backslash, emoji, multi-line and 4 KiB long) set once appears with exactly the same bytes in `pb exec`, in a new `pb connect` terminal, and in a `pb ssh` command session on each OS of the pair (check with `printenv`/`env` on Unix and `Get-ChildItem Env:`/`set` on W, compared by sha256 of each value); nothing is translated (a `PATH`-like value keeps `:` or `;` as written, no drive-letter or slash rewriting). | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-12 | ENV scopes across OSes: a per-device override set for a W machine reaches only that machine; personal and team scopes reach L, M and W machines of the same account or team; a team scope granted to a member whose machine is another OS is delivered there and removed there on revoke; rotation after a revoked machine reaches the remaining machines of every OS; `pb env host show` states delivery truthfully on each OS. Windows treats variable names case-insensitively; defined and built: saving a name that differs only by case from another name of the same scope is refused when the scope reaches a Windows machine (message names both, nothing is written), Linux and macOS scopes stay case-sensitive, and the server cannot check it because names are encrypted (client check, `TestWindowsScopeRefusesNamesDifferingOnlyByCase`). Still to define and check on a W machine: a name differing only by case in two different scopes (for example personal `Path` and a device override `PATH`). | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-13 | ENV vault across OSes: a vault created on one OS unlocks on a device of another OS using the password or recovery key (keychain on M, Credential Manager on W, Secret Service or password on L); locking, reset and remove on one device are honoured on the others; the vault never writes values into logs, diagnostics, shell history or the config file on any OS. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-14 | File send and Inbox between OSes, names: `pb send` of names that are valid on the sender but not on the receiver (`a:b.txt`, trailing dot or space, `CON.txt`, `A.txt` next to `a.txt`, a 250-byte name, NFC vs NFD, leading `-`) either arrives under a clearly reported safe name or is refused with a message naming the file and the reason; no silent overwrite, no path escape (`..`, absolute path, drive letter), received bytes equal (sha256) in both directions. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-15 | File send and Inbox, permissions and trees: a folder keeps its structure (nested, empty directories, the 10 files / 500 MiB limits), file modes on the receiver follow the receiver's policy (documented: no setuid, no executable surprise; executable intent only where the rule says so), symlinks inside a folder are refused or skipped the same way on every OS, an interrupted transfer resumes and finishes identically across the pair, and a team file request approved on one OS is received on another. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-16 | Terminal from each OS to each other OS (`pb connect`, both directions): the target's default shell starts (bash, zsh or PowerShell), UTF-8 and wide characters render, window resize reaches the remote PTY, Ctrl-C and Ctrl-D behave, a long-running command survives detach and reconnect with replay, the exit code is reported, and a shared terminal joined from the other OS shows the same output. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-17 | Exec from each OS to each other OS (`pb exec`, both directions): exact argv is preserved (spaces, quotes, empty arguments), `--cwd` takes the target's path form (`/home/u` vs `C:\Users\u`) and a path of the other form is refused clearly, `--env` entries arrive, stdout and stderr stay separate, exit codes and signals map, `--pty` works, output above 1 MiB (8 MiB tested) arrives byte-exact, and an offline target gives the documented message. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-18 | SSH family between OSes (`pb ssh`, `scp`, `sftp`, `rsync`, from each OS to each other, both directions): host identity and known-hosts handling per OS, key custody per OS, path forms for remote paths (drive letters, backslashes) accepted or refused clearly, file bytes equal in both directions, rsync works where available and fails with a clear message on W without it, and `pb ssh` to the same machine is refused clearly on every OS. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-19 | Private access between OSes: raw TCP through `127.100.x.y` and `pb access machine` is byte-exact with half-close in both directions for each OS pair, machine names resolve without editing hosts or resolver files, `https://<machine>.<domain>:<port>` and `https://<port>.<machine>.<domain>` work with the local CA trusted through each OS's own store (Windows certificate store, macOS keychain, NSS or system store on Linux), and revoking a team grant or the team setting ends flows across OS. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-20 | Previews and tunnels from machines of each OS: a preview or tunnel whose origin listens on `127.0.0.1` only, on `::1` only (Windows resolves `localhost` to IPv6 first) and on all addresses is reachable through the public URL; HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2, WebSocket, SSE, large downloads and raw TCP behave the same; the browser opens the URL from each OS; stopping the origin gives the same error page everywhere. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-21 | Team sharing across OSes: a machine of OS A shared into a team and used by a member whose own device is OS B: terminal join and shared-terminal viewer/interactive roles, exec, files, private access, ENV scopes, tunnel and preview management all follow the grant; revoke, unshare, member removal and team suspension end active sessions within the documented time on every OS pair; machine admission (approve new machines) works when the member enrolls an OS different from the owner's. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |
| X-22 | Same-account mixed fleet end to end: one account with an L, an M and a W machine runs the whole daily loop on each pair (enroll, name resolution, `pb exec` everywhere, config sync with one shared TOML, ENV, send a file both ways, private TCP, a preview), then a reboot of one machine and a network change on another, and recovers without user action; `pb doctor` is healthy on all three and the console shows every machine online with its OS. | [ ] | [ ] | [ ] | — |

## F22. File transfer modes (PLAN 3b)

| ID | Scenario | L | M | W | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F22-01 | Two modes: Automatic (default, at most 2,000,000,000 bytes per send, P2P else any relay incl. hosted, hosted bytes counted) and Unlimited (no size bound, P2P or self-hosted relays only, pauses with "Waiting for a direct connection or your relay" and resumes; resume survives pb and daemon restarts). | [x] | [ ] | [ ] | Owner: Lane A (CLI/daemon), Lane O (server policy, claims, preferences, progress), Lane R (PathEligibility), Lane C (console). Checks: size boundary and batch total (TestAutomaticSendBoundIsTheBatchTotal, cbe0dfbe); policy accepts automatic_max_batch_bytes (TestPolicyAcceptsTheAutomaticBatchBound, 77d8a1bc); to write: unlimited never touches a hosted relay (fake path flips to hosted_relay; test fails on any byte), pause/resume on path loss, resume after daemon restart, destination refuses unlimited on hosted relay. 2026-10-26 L PASS (Transfers lane, dev-7b6b91be, pbvm2 containers): tools/device-smoke/transfer_modes.sh: Automatic 120 MiB and a 600 MiB six-file batch arrive byte-identical; a 2.2 GB send is refused before data moves ('Automatic sends up to 2 GB'); Unlimited 300 MiB with the client's UDP blocked prints 'Waiting for a direct connection or your relay' (no TTY) and delivers nothing, then resumes byte-identical; with UDP lost at 60 MB the sender's egress over the next 30 s stays under 3 MB (nothing crosses the hosted relay), its daemon restarts while it waits, and it resumes byte-identical. Found and fixed on the way: b5dc5845 (pb send hard-coded transfer_mode=automatic since 04ecd8c5, so --unlimited never guarded the path), 7b6b91be (a paused send stayed paused after its daemon restarted: the peer reads as no path until traffic crosses). Also eff7bf24: destination_update_required text and 'pb send list' needing --on read as advice. M and W unchanged; a destination that refuses Unlimited on a hosted relay has no device check yet. |

## R. Release safety (launch gate)

Nothing reaches users that a machine and a server have not run together first. These rows gate the launch; the progress page shows them as their own card. Owners are proposed; the coordinator confirms them.

| ID | Scenario | L | M | W | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-01 | No direct pushes to main: every change lands through a pull request and the required CI checks must pass before merge; the branch rule applies to everyone, including admins. | [ ] | — | — | Owner: Lane G (CI) + coordinator (repository settings). Check: the GitHub branch protection (read through the API) lists the required checks, and a direct push to main is refused (recorded attempt). |
| R-02 | An end-to-end suite runs in CI on every pull request: a real server, an edge, a relay and two machines pair, exec, send a file and open a preview, and the job fails on any step. | [ ] | — | — | Owner: Lane G with Lane B (server, edge) and Lane A (machines). Check: the CI job name in the required checks; a deliberately broken change turns it red. |
| R-03 | An upgrade matrix runs before a release: old and new client x old and new server x old and new edge, and one machine kept connected through a rolling server and edge restart (no dropped session, no failed check). | [ ] | — | — | Owner: Lane P (builds) with Lane G (CI). Check: the matrix result file per release (every cell PASS), including the downgrade case. The 2026-10-26 downgrade bug (carrier crash loop with doctor healthy) is a cell that must fail first. |
| R-04 | Deploys go staging first, then a canary, with automatic rollback: a new server or edge build runs on staging, then on one production node, and is rolled back by itself when error rate, latency or the synthetic probes (R-06) go outside their bounds. | [ ] | — | — | Owner: Lane B with Lane G. Check: a drill that deploys a build which fails its probes and is rolled back with no manual step, plus the rollout log. |
| R-05 | Client release channels dev, beta and stable: a build is promoted one channel at a time, a machine follows only its channel, and a bad build is pulled from a channel without touching the others. | [ ] | — | — | Owner: Lane A (update client) with Lane P (builds) and Lane G (publishing). Check: test that a beta machine does not take a dev build and a stable machine does not take a beta build; a pulled build is not offered again. |
| R-06 | Production synthetic probes run every minute (sign-in, pair, exec between two probe machines, a file send, a public preview and tunnel request) and alert on failure within five minutes. | [ ] | — | — | Owner: Lane B with Lane W (probe machines). Check: the probe history on the status page shows one result per minute for 24 h, and a forced failure raises the alert. |
